              "text": "pool/PART.gate@2/gate.c, lines 1 to 400 of 704\n1\t/* SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0-only\n2\t *\n3\t * PART.gate@2: the gate, and the system calls a normal Linux program makes.\n4\t * It is @1, unchanged for every program @1 ran, plus (part 20, findings 0023\n5\t * item 10): a program loaded from a Linux program file (gate_exec), whose\n6\t * window may sit at any virtual address and maps to its own physical RAM, and\n7\t * the calls Berkeley's cat makes through musl, each at Linux's 386 number and\n8\t * each as small as cat needs: open and close (read-only, the disk's root\n9\t * directory), read and write (the console, or an open file), writev, brk,\n10\t * mmap2, munmap, set_thread_area, set_tid_address, statx, ioctl and\n11\t * exit_group.  Anything a call is asked for that it does not do is refused with\n12\t * Linux's error number, never half done.  Ctrl+D ends what is typed, and a\n13\t * Linux program's exit status is said on the console.\n14\t *\n15\t * What follows is @1's header, still true:\n16\t *\n17\t * Programs run in ring 3, the processor's least\n18\t * privileged level, and the only way they reach the kernel is a trap\n19\t * instruction, int 0x80, with the call's number in eax.  A program that\n20\t * touches anything else (the kernel's memory, the screen's, an I/O port) is\n21\t * stopped by the processor itself, and the kernel says so on the console.\n22\t * Plan 9.13, findings 0021.  Ours; nothing lifted.  The descriptor, page-table\n23\t * and task-state-segment bits are the Intel SDM's; the register convention of\n24\t * int 0x80 and the numbers of write, read and exit are Linux's 32-bit x86 ABI.\n25\t *\n26\t * Memory: every address maps to itself.  The kernel's pages, and every page\n27\t * outside the program's window, are closed to ring 3; only the window, the\n28\t * program's stack and the page of system call stubs (gate_user.S) are open.\n29\t * The kernel obeys read-only pages too (CR0.WP).\n30\t *\n31\t * Several programs (PART.share@2): each gets a space, its own page directory\n32\t * that maps the same virtual window to its own physical RAM, and its own kernel\n33\t * stack, where the processor puts a trap's frame. They go when part 20 brings a loader for Linux's\n54\t   program files: alloc becomes brk, ms becomes clock_gettime, load and save\n55\t   become open, read, write and close.  Until then a Linux program that asks\n56\t   for one of those four gets an error, not a guess.\n57\t\n58\t   TECH-DEBT 2.  A system call runs with interrupts off, so a program waiting\n59\t   inside `read` for a byte stops every other program too, and the stopwatch\n60\t   freezes with it.  Left on purpose.  The fix (let the timer tick in while the\n61\t   read waits) is already written and is the first post of the mechanic, after\n62\t   the series.  No test runs two programs with one of them waiting for input.\n63\t\n64\t   TECH-DEBT 3.  A program's window is rounded up to whole 4 MiB pages, so when\n65\t   the end of its memory is not on a 4 MiB line, the rest of that page is open\n66\t   to it as well: memory it was never given.  Left on purpose.  The mechanic\n67\t   closes it, after the series, by aligning the window or using 4 KiB pages at\n68\t   the edge.\n69\t   TECH-DEBT 4.  (Added in part 20, findings 0023 item 10, decision 0046.)  The\n70\t   16 bytes PART.loader@10 hands a Linux program as AT_RANDOM come from the\n71\t   processor's cycle counter: they are not random, and nothing on AiOs is yet.\n72\t   musl uses them for its stack canary and nothing else here; cat is built\n73\t   without one. The gate now holds the page directories (one\n82\t   per program, the same virtual window mapped to that program's own RAM), the\n83\t   interrupt table with the 8259's vectors, a 16 KiB kernel stack per program\n84\t   where the processor puts a trap's frame, and the saving and resuming of\n85\t   frames.  Share keeps only the turns: the tick, what to do when a program\n86\t   exits or is stopped, and the cpio reader.  A new flavor, gateshare\n87\t   (petitions 0004, 0006 and 0011), runs the same stopwatch, sieve and probes\n88\t   as the share flavor on PART.loader@9. 0 0\n96\t}\n97\t\n98\t# every catalogue number the flavor's petitions name that has no catalogue entry, one to\n99\t# a line with what kind of thing it is; sorted, so the same flavor gives the same list\n100\tcmd_missing() {\n101\t\tf=$(flavor_file \"$1\")\n102\t\tfor id in $(list_field \"$f\" petitions); do petition_codes \"petitions/$id.toml\"; done | LC_ALL=C sort -u | while read -r c; do\n103\t\t\t[ -e \"$(code_file \"$c\")\" ] && continue\n104\t\t\tcase \"${c%%.*}\" in\n105\t\t\t\tPUR) k=purpose ;; KEY) k=key ;; CAP) k=capability ;; PART) k=part ;; MACHINE) k=machine ;;\n106\t\t\t\tTOOL) k=tool ;; AGT) k=agent ;; SPEC) k=clause ;; TST) k=test ;; SNS) k=sensor ;; FND) k=finding ;;\n107\t\t\t\t*) k=thing ;;\n108\t\t\tesac\n109\t\t\tprintf '%s  %s not in the catalogue\\n' \"$c\" \"$k\"\n110\t\tdone\n111\t}\n112\t\n113\t# prog_flags CODE: the extra compile flags a program's catalogue entry lists in\n114\t# cflags (SPEC.builder.3@1), one to a line, -include=H as -include and H; any\n115\t# flag outside the allowed forms stops the build, naming the program and the flag\n116\tprog_flags() {\n117\t\tfor x in $(list_field \"$(code_file \"$1\")\" cflags); do\n118\t\t\tif printf '%s' \"$x\" | grep -Eqx -- '-std=[a-z0-9]+|-D[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*(\\([A-Za-z_]+\\))?(=[A-Za-z0-9_]*)?'; then\n119\t\t\t\tprintf '%s\\n' \"$x\"\n120\t\t\telif printf '%s' \"$x\" | grep -Eqx -- '-include=[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_/.@-]*\\.h' && ! printf '%s' \"$x\" | grep -q '\\.\\.'; then\n121\t\t\t\tprintf -- '-include\\n%s\\n' \"${x#-include=}\"\n122\t\t\telse\n123\t\t\t\tdie \"SPEC.builder.3: $1 lists the compile flag '$x', which is not -std=, -D or -include= of a header\"\n124\t\t\tfi\n125\t\tdone\n126\t}\n127\t\n128\t# libc_musl DIR: musl from lifted/PART.libc.musl@1, built the way jig/libc builds\n129\t# the pinned one: the two generated headers from their lifted inputs, every\n130\t# lifted source compiled with musl's own flags for i386, the library, the start\n131\t# files, the headers a program sees, and the divide helper musl needs\n132\t# (PART.divide64@1).  Only what was lifted is there to build.\n133\tlibc_musl() {\n134\t\tm=lifted/PART.libc.musl@1; d=$1\n135\t\t[ -f \"$d/libc.a\" ] && return 0\n136\t\tmkdir -p \"$d/obj/include/bits\" \"$d/include/bits\"\n137\t\tsed -f \"$m/tools/mkalltypes.sed\" \"$m/arch/i386/bits/alltypes.h.in\" \"$m/include/alltypes.h.in\" > \"$d/obj/include/bits/alltypes.h\"\n138\t\tcp \"$m/arch/i386/bits/syscall.h.in\" \"$d/obj/include/bits/syscall.h\"\n139\t\tsed -n -e s/__NR_/SYS_/p < \"$m/arch/i386/bits/syscall.h.in\" >> \"$d/obj/include/bits/syscall.h\"\n140\t\tmf=\"-m32 -std=c99 -nostdinc -ffreestanding -fexcess-precision=standard -frounding-math -fno-strict-aliasing -Wa,--noexecstack -D_XOPEN_SOURCE=700 -I$m/arch/i386 -I$m/arch/generic -I$d/obj/src/internal -I$m/src/include -I$m/src/internal -I$d/obj/include -I$m/include -Os -fomit-frame-pointer -fno-unwind-tables -fno-asynchronous-unwind-tables -ffunction-sections -fdata-sections -march=i486 -mtune=generic -fno-pie -w\"\n141\t\t( cd \"$m\" && find src -type f \\( -name '*.c' -o -name '*.s' \\) ) | LC_ALL=C sort | while read -r f; do\n142\t\t\to=\"$d/obj/$(echo \"$f\" | tr / @).o\"\n143\t\t\tx=\"\"; case \"$f\" in */__libc_start_main.c|*/__init_tls.c|*/__set_thread_area.s|*/memset.s|*/memcpy.s) x=\"-fno-stack-protector\" ;; esac\n144\t\t\t$CC $mf $x -c -o \"$o\" \"$m/$f\" || exit 1\n145\t\tdone || die \"musl from lifted/ did not compile\"\n146\t\t( cd \"$d/obj\" && ls *.o | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs ar rcs ../libc.a )\n147\t\t$CC $mf -DCRT -fno-stack-protector -c -o \"$d/crt1.o\" \"$m/crt/crt1.c\"\n148\t\t$CC $mf -c -o \"$d/crti.o\" \"$m/crt/i386/crti.s\"\n149\t\t$CC $mf -c -o \"$d/crtn.o\" \"$m/crt/i386/crtn.s\"\n150\t\t$CC -m32 -O2 -ffreestanding -fno-pie -c -o \"$d/divide64.o\" pool/PART.divide64@1/divide64.c\n151\t\t( cd \"$m/include\" && find . -type f ! -name '*.in' ) | while read -r h; do mkdir -p \"$d/include/$(dirname \"$h\")\"; cp \"$m/include/$h\" \"$d/include/$h\"; done\n152\t\tcp \"$m\"/arch/generic/bits/*.h \"$d/include/bits/\"; cp \"$m\"/arch/i386/bits/*.h \"$d/include/bits/\"\n153\t\tcp \"$d/obj/include/bits/alltypes.h\" \"$d/obj/include/bits/syscall.h\" \"$d/include/bits/\"\n154\t}\n155\t\n156\t# a petition is built only when a human decided it: a decision in decisions/, by a\n157\t# key in constitution/keys/, that names the petition's own file in its touches.\n158\t# Exact names only, so no decision decides petitions not yet written (decision 0051).\n159\tdecided() {\t# PETITION_FILE\n160\t\tfor d in decisions/*.toml; do\n161\t\t\tby=$(field \"$d\" by)\n162\t\t\t[ -f \"constitution/keys/${by#KEY.}.toml\" ] || continue\n163\t\t\tset -f\t# a touches entry is a pattern, not file names: with globbing on,\n164\t\t\t\t# petitions/* became every petition there is (findings 0017 #7)\n165\t\t\tfor g in $(list_field \"$d\" touches); do\n166\t\t\t\t[ \"$g\" = \"$1\" ] && { set +f; return 0; }\n167\t\t\tdone\n168\t\t\tset +f\n169\t\tdone\n170\t\treturn 1\n171\t}\n172\t\n173\tcmd_build() {\n174\t\tfor id in $(list_field \"$(flavor_file \"$1\")\" petitions); do\n175\t\t\tdecided \"petitions/$id.toml\" || die \"SPEC.builder.4: petition $id is named by no decision under a key in constitution/keys/; a human has not decided it\"\n176\t\tdone\n177\t\tmiss=$(cmd_missing \"$1\")\n178\t\tif [ -n \"$miss\" ]; then\n179\t\t\tmkdir -p \"out/$1\"; printf '%s\\n' \"$miss\" > \"out/$1/missing.txt\"\n180\t\t\tprintf 'stopped: %s catalogue numbers are missing; the list is out/%s/missing.txt\\n' \"$(printf '%s\\n' \"$miss\" | wc -l | tr -d ' ')\" \"$1\" >&2\n181\t\t\texit 3\n182\t\tfi\n183\t\trm -f \"out/$1/missing.txt\" 2> /dev/null || :\n184\t\t# every program's own flags are checked before anything is compiled\n185\t\tfor id in $(list_field \"$(flavor_file \"$1\")\" petitions); do p=$(petition_primary \"petitions/$id.toml\"); prog_flags \"$p\" > /dev/null || exit 1; done\n186\t\tsh pool/AGT.referee@1/referee check \"$1\" >/dev/null\n187\t\tf=$(flavor_file \"$1\")\n188\t\tprogs=$(for id in $(list_field \"$f\" petitions); do petition_primary \"petitions/$id.toml\"; done)\n189\t\t# walk run-time depends from the programs down; the loader is the newest any of them depends on\n190\t\tfor prog in $progs; do [ -n \"$(list_field \"$(code_file \"$prog\")\" depends)\" ] || die \"rule 17: $prog depends on nothing; a program needs a loader\"; done\n191\t\tloader=$(kernel_for \"$f\" $progs)\n192\t\tlf=$(code_file \"$loader\"); ldir=$(field \"$lf\" source)\n193\t\tout=\"out/$1\"; mkdir -p \"$out\"\n194\t\tCC=${CC:-gcc}\n195\t\tCFLAGS=\"-m32 -std=c11 -ffreestanding -fno-pic -fno-pie -fno-stack-protector -fno-builtin -fno-asynchronous-unwind-tables -mno-sse -mno-mmx -mno-red-zone -mgeneral-regs-only -nostdlib -nostdinc -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Werror\"\n196\t\tLDFLAGS=\"-static -no-pie -nostartfiles -Wl,--build-id=none -Wl,-z,noexecstack\"\n197\t\tif [ -n \"$(list_field \"$lf\" builds_with)\" ]; then\n198\t\t\t# plan 5.8, the petition is the partition: the kernel's own depends, plus\n199\t\t\t# every part the flavor's petitions name that it builds with, plus what\n200\t\t\t# those depend on, one version of each (the newest named), and nothing else\n201\t\t\twant=$( { list_field \"$lf\" depends\n202\t\t\t\tfor id in $(list_field \"$f\" petitions); do petition_parts \"petitions/$id.toml\"; done | while read -r c; do\n203\t\t\t\t\tlist_field \"$lf\" builds_with | grep -qx \"$c\" && echo \"$c\"; done; } | sort -u)\n204\t\t\twhile :; do\n205\t\t\t\tmore=$(for c in $want; do list_field \"$(code_file \"$c\")\" depends; done | sort -u)\n206\t\t\t\tnext=$(printf '%s\\n' $want $more | grep . | sort -u)\n207\t\t\t\t[ \"$next\" = \"$want\" ] && break; want=$next\n208\t\t\tdone\n209\t\t\tparts=\"\"; do [ -d \"$d\" ] || die \"rule 18: source $d is not in the pool\"; done\n221\t\tobjs=\"\"\n222\t\t# the parts, then the kernel's own .S then .c: the link order every earlier kernel was built in\n223\t\tfor src in $(for d in $ddirs; do for x in \"$d\"*.c \"$d\"*.S; do [ -e \"$x\" ] && echo \"$x\"; done; done) $(for x in \"$ldir\"*.S \"$ldir\"*.c; do [ -e \"$x\" ] && echo \"$x\"; done); do\n224\t\t\to=\"$out/$(basename \"${src%.*}\").o\"; $CC $CFLAGS $haves $incs -c -o \"$o\" \"$src\"; objs=\"$objs $o\"\n225\t\tdone\n226\t\t$CC $CFLAGS $LDFLAGS -T \"$ldir/loader.ld\" -o \"$out/loader.elf\" $objs\n227\t\t# each program is built against its own loader's program format, unchanged\n228\t\tmembers=\"\"; summary=\"\"\n229\t\tfor prog in $progs; do\n230\t\t\tpdir=$(field \"$(code_file \"$prog\")\" source)\n231\t\t\t[ -d \"$pdir\" ] || die \"rule 18: source $pdir is not in the pool\"\n232\t\t\tif list_field \"$(code_file \"$prog\")\" depends | grep -qx 'PART.libc.musl@1'; then\n233\t\t\t\t# a normal Linux program: built against musl from lifted/, linked the\n234\t\t\t\t# way a Linux toolchain links a static program, an ELF file (part 20)\n235\t\t\t\tpname=$(basename \"${prog#PART.}\"); pname=${pname%@*}\n236\t\t\t\tlibc_musl \"$out/musl\"\n237\t\t\t\tpf=$(prog_flags \"$prog\" | tr '\\n' ' '); pq=$(prog_flags \"$prog\" | sed \"s/.*/'&'/\" | tr '\\n' ' ')\n238\t\t\t\tmflags=\"-m32 -march=i486 -O2 -nostdinc -fno-pie -fno-stack-protector -I $out/musl/include\"\n239\t\t\t\t$CC $mflags $pf -c -o \"$out/$pname.o\" \"$pdir$pname.c\"\n240\t\t\t\tCC=\"$CC\" CFLAGS=\"$mflags $pq\" sh pool/AGT.referee@1/referee hardware \"$prog\" \"$pdir$pname.c\"\n241\t\t\t\t$CC -m32 -static -nostdlib -no-pie -Wl,--build-id=none -Wl,-z,noexecstack -o \"$out/$pname.bin\" \\\n242\t\t\t\t\t\"$out/musl/crt1.o\" \"$out/musl/crti.o\" \"$out/$pname.o\" \"$out/musl/libc.a\" \"$out/musl/divide64.o\" \"$out/musl/crtn.o\"\n243\t\t\t\tmembers=\"$members $pname $out/$pname.bin\"; pname=${pname%@*}\n249\t\t\tpf=$(prog_flags \"$prog\" | tr '\\n' ' ')\n250\t\t\t# the referee hands CFLAGS to a shell, so there each flag goes in quotes (none can hold one)\n251\t\t\tpq=$(prog_flags \"$prog\" | sed \"s/.*/'&'/\" | tr '\\n' ' ')\n252\t\t\t$CC $CFLAGS $pf -c -o \"$out/$pname.o\" \"$pdir$pname.c\"\n253\t\t\t# the referee, after: rule 12, a program touches no hardware\n254\t\t\tCC=\"$CC\" CFLAGS=\"$CFLAGS $pq\" sh pool/AGT.referee@1/referee hardware \"$prog\" \"$pdir$pname.c\"\n255\t\t\t$CC $CFLAGS $LDFLAGS -T \"$pldir/program.ld\" -o \"$out/$pname.bin\" \"$out/$pname.o\"\n256\t\t\tmembers=\"$members $pname $out/$pname.bin\"; summary=\"$summary + $prog ($(wc -c < \"$out/$pname.bin\") bytes)\"\n257\t\tdone\n258\t\tif [ \"$(echo $progs | wc -w)\" -gt 1 ]; then\n259\t\t\tnewc \"$out/programs.bin\" $members\n260\t\t\tpname=programs\n261\t\tfi\n262\t\tprintf 'built flavor %s: %s (%s)%s -> %s/\\n' \"$1\" \"$loader\" \"$(sha256sum < \"$out/loader.elf\" | cut -c1-16)\" \"$summary\" \"$out\"\n263\t\techo \"$pname\" > \"$out/program\"\n264\t}\n265\t\n266\tcmd_run() {\n267\t\tcmd_build \"$1\"\n268\t\tf=$(flavor_file \"$1\"); mdir=$(field \"$(code_file \"$(field \"$f\" machine)\")\" source)\n269\t\tpname=$(cat \"out/$1/program\")\n270\t\texec sh \"$mdir/machine.sh\" \"out/$1/loader.elf\" \"out/$1/$pname.bin\"\n271\t}\n272\t\n273\tcase \"${1:-}\" in\n274\t\tlist)  cmd_list ;;\n275\t\tcheck|rules|lines|ledger) exec sh pool/AGT.referee@1/referee \"$@\" ;;\t# the referee, for old habits\n276\t\tmissing) cmd_missing \"${2:?flavor}\" ;;\n277\t\tbuild) cmd_build \"${2:?flavor}\" ;;\n278\t\trun)   cmd_run \"${2:?flavor}\" ;;\n279\t\tpack)  shift; newc \"$@\" ;;\n280\t\t*) sed -n '2,15p' \"$0\"; exit 2 ;;\n281\tesac"
            }
          ],
          "status": "success"
        }

PUR.0003: a reverse-Polish calculator at the serial console.\n4\t * Type numbers and operators, it keeps a stack, shows the top after each line,\n5\t * and quits on q. Returns 0 to stop the line on an error. */\n89\tstatic int token(const char *t, unsigned long n)\n90\t{\n91\t\tint v, a, b;\n92\t\n93\t\tswitch (number(t, n, &v)) {\n94\t\tcase 1:\n95\t\t\tif (depth == DEPTH) { put(\"error: stack full\\n\"); return 1; }\n104\t\tif (same(t, n, \"drop\")) { if (!need(1)) return 0; depth--; return 1; }\n105\t\tif (same(t, n, \"dup\"))  {\n106\t\t\tif (!need(1)) return 0;\n107\t\t\tif (depth == DEPTH) { put(\"error: stack full\\n\"); return 0; }\n108\t\t\tstack[depth] = stack[depth - 1]; depth++; return 1;\n109\t\t}\n110\t\tif (same(t, n, \"swap\")) {\n111\t\t\tif (!need(2)) return 0;\n112\t\t\tv = stack[depth - 1]; stack[depth - 1] = stack[depth - 2]; b = stack[depth - 1];\n118\t\t\tif ((t[0] == '/' || t[0] == '%') && b == 0) { put(\"error: divide by zero\\n\"); return 0; }\n119\t\t\tif ((t[0] == '/' || t[0] == '%') && a == -2147483647 - 1 && b == -1) { put(\"error: overflow\\n\"); return 0; }\n120\t\t\tswitch (t[0]) {\n121\t\t\tcase '+': v = (int)((unsigned int)a + (unsigned int)b); break;\n122\t\t\tcase '-': v = (int)((unsigned int)a - (unsigned int)b); break;\n123\t\t\tcase '*': v = (int)((unsigned int)a * (unsigned int)b); break;\n124\t\t\tcase '/': v = a / b; break;\n125\t\t\tdefault:  v = a % b; break;\n126\t\t\t}\n127\t\t\tdepth--;\n128\t\t\tstack[depth - 1] = v;\n129\t\t\treturn 1;\n130\t\t}\n131\t\tput(\"error: unknown '\"); out(t, n); put(\"'\\n\");\n132\t\treturn 0;\n133\t}\n134\t\n135\tstatic void line(const char *s, unsigned long n)\n136\t{\n137\t\tunsigned long i = 0, start;\n138\t\n139\t\twhile (i < n) {\n140\t\t\twhile (i < n && s[i] == ' ') i++;\n141\t\t\tif (i == n) break;\n142\t\t\tstart = i;\n143\t\t\twhile (i < n && s[i] != ' ') i++;\n144\t\t\tif (!token(s + start, i - start)) return;\n145\t\t}\n146\t\tif (depth) { put(\"= \");         "text": "Now I need to understand: when life@2 runs under `PART.gate@2` (the cat flavor), how does `door->alloc()` work?\n\nIn the `gate_run()` path, the program is given a pointer to `gate_door` (from `gate_user.S`), which is a function table. The `d_alloc` stub calls `int $0x80` with eax=1000. So it does go through the NR_ALLOC case.\n\nBut wait - the ladder test for gate.ladder-2 runs programs that are built for their OWN loaders. These programs use the `door->alloc()` function from the table they were given. When booted under the cat flavor's loader (PART.loader@10 with PART.gate@2), the program gets the `gate_door` stub table, and `d_alloc` uses syscall 1000.\n\nSo if I remove NR_ALLOC from PART.gate@2, the `life` program would call alloc → stub → int 0x80 with eax=1000 → NR_ALLOC no longer matched → return -ENOSYS → and life would say \"alloc refused\" and fail.\n\nSo the fix needs to be more subtle. The spec says \"no number is AiOs's own (TECH-DEBT 1, findings 0021 item 5)\". The `gate_user.S` stubs need to be updated too - `d_alloc` should use brk (45), `d_ms` should use clock_gettime (265), etc. But wait - the test only checks 1000-1003 specifically.\n\nLet me re-read the failing test output more carefully:\n\n```\nFAIL  TST.gate.linux-only@1  1s  an AiOs-only number is still answered: > nr-1000 0 > nr-1001 0 > nr-1002 -14 > nr-1003 -14\n```\n\n- nr-1000 returned 0 (instead of -38)\n- nr-1001 returned 0 (instead of -38)  \n- nr-1002 returned -14 = -EFAULT (instead of -38)\n- nr-1003 returned -14 = -EFAULT (instead of -38)\n\nThe debt.c program calls `sys(1000, 0, 0, 0)`, `sys(1001, 0, 0, 0)`, `sys(1002, 0, 0, 0)`, `sys(1003, 0, 0, 0)`. The test is run through `gate_exec` path (it's booted as an ELF Linux program), which means `brk_lo` is set.\n\nLooking at gate.c:\n- NR_ALLOC (1000): `return (long)calls->alloc(a)` → alloc(0) returns some pointer, so 0 or a positive value\n- NR_MS (1001): `return (long)calls->ms()` → ms() returns 0 \n- NR_LOAD (1002): `return calls->load(...)` → EFAULT because `a=0`, and `!user_name_ok((const char *)a)` → -EFAULT\n- NR_SAVE (1003): same as load → -EFAULT\n\nNow, the spec says when Linux program files run (part 20, i.e., `PART.gate@2`), these AiOs-only numbers should no longer be answered — they should return -ENOSYS.\n\nThe key insight is: `gate_exec` is the path for Linux programs (those loaded from ELF files). When `gate_exec` is used, `brk_lo` is non-zero. The AiOs programs (`gate_run` path) set `brk_lo = 0`.\n\nLooking at the code, when a Linux program is run via `gate_exec`, `brk_lo` is set. When an old-style AiOs program is run via `gate_run`, `brk_lo` is 0.\n\nThe fix should: when running a Linux program (brk_lo != 0), return -ENOSYS for the AiOs-specific syscalls 1000-1003. But when running the old-style programs (brk_lo == 0), keep the existing behavior.\n\nWait, but there's already code for this pattern in the existing cases! For example:\n- `NR_MUNMAP`: `return brk_lo ? 0 : -ENOSYS;`\n- `NR_SET_TID_ADDRESS`: `return brk_lo ? 1 : -ENOSYS;`\n- `NR_MMAP2`, `NR_BRK`, `NR_SET_THREAD_AREA`, `NR_STATX`: check `if (!brk_lo) return -ENOSYS;`\n\nSo the pattern is: if `brk_lo` is zero, the program is an old-style AiOs program (like hello/hanoi/life/calc), and for those we need the alloc/ms/load/save. If `brk_lo` is non-zero, it's a Linux ELF program, and those should get -ENOSYS.\n\nBut actually, looking at the current code for NR_ALLOC/NR_MS/NR_LOAD/NR_SAVE, there's no brk_lo check. The fix would be to add a check: if `brk_lo` is set (Linux program), return -ENOSYS for 1000-1003. If `brk_lo` is NOT set (old AiOs program), keep existing behavior.\n\nLet me verify by checking the `gate.ladder-2@1` test runs under cat flavor (which uses gate_exec and has brk_lo != 0) and life@2 uses alloc... wait, but in the `gate.ladder-2@1` test, life is run through the loader as an old-style program, using `gate_run` (not `gate_exec`), right?\n\nLet me re-read the test description: \"hello, hanoi, life or calc, built for its own loader and booted under the cat flavor's PART.loader@10 and PART.gate@2, does not print exactly what it always has\"\n\nSo they're booted via `PART.loader@10`. Linux i386 */\n#define NR_READ\t\t3\n#define NR_WRITE\t4\n#define NR_ALLOC\t1000\t\t/* old AiOs-only number; Linux programs get -ENOSYS (see TECH-DEBT 1 above) */\n#define NR_MS\t\t1001\t\t/* old AiOs-only number; Linux programs get -ENOSYS */\n#define NR_LOAD\t\t1002\t\t/* old AiOs-only number; Linux programs get -ENOSYS */\n#define NR_SAVE\t\t1003\t\t/* old AiOs-only number; Linux programs get -ENOSYS */\n#define NR_OPEN\t\t5\n#define NR_CLOSE\t6\t\t/* Linux i386, from here on: the calls a normal Linux program makes */\n#define NR_BRK\t\t45\n#define NR_IOCTL\t54\n#define NR_MUNMAP\t91\n#define NR_WRITEV\t146\n#define NR_MMAP2\t192\n#define NR_SET_THREAD_AREA 243\n#define NR_EXIT_GROUP\t252\n#define NR_SET_TID_ADDRESS 258\n#define NR_STATX\t383\n\n#define ENOENT\t\t2\n#define ENOMEM\t\t12\n#define EMFILE\t\t24\n#define EROFS\t\t30\n#define EINVAL\t\t22\n#define ENOTTY\t\t25\n#define AT_EMPTY_PATH\t0x1000\n#define MAP_PRIVATE\t0x02\n#define MAP_ANONYMOUS\t0x20\n#define SEL_TLS\t\t0x30\t\t/* the one thread-storage segment a program may set, GDT entry 6 */\n#define TLS_ENTRY\t6\n#define PAGE\t\t4096UL\n#define NFILES\t\t4\t\t/* files a Linux program may have open at once, from descriptor 3 */\n#define FILE_MAX\t8192\t\t/* the most a file can hold on the disk as lifted (seven 1 KiB zones), and then some */\n\n#define EFAULT\t\t14\n#define EBADF\t\t9\n#define ENOSYS\t\t38\n#define NAME_MAX_LEN\t256\t\t/* the longest file name the kernel will read from a program */\n\nextern char kernel_end[], stack_top[];\nextern char gate_user_start[], gate_stack_lo[], gate_stack_hi[];\nextern char gate_door[], gate_return[];\n\n#define KSTACK_WORDS\t4096\t\t/* 16 KiB of kernel stack for each space */\n\nstruct gate_space {\n\tunsigned long cr3;\t\t/* its page directory */\n\tunsigned long *frame;\t\t/* where its registers were saved, on its kernel stack */\n\tunsigned long kstack_top;\t/* where a trap from ring 3 lands (the TSS's esp0) */\n\tint user_stack;\t\t\t/* ring 3 stands on the gate's own stack (one program, no sharing) */\n};\n\nstatic unsigned long long gdt[7];\nstatic unsigned int tss[26];\nstatic unsigned long long idt[256];\nstatic unsigned long pds[GATE_MAX_SPACES][1024] __attribute__((aligned(4096)));\nstatic unsigned long pt0[1024] __attribute__((aligned(4096)));\nstatic unsigned long kstacks[GATE_MAX_SPACES][KSTACK_WORDS] __attribute__((aligned(16)));\nstatic struct gate_space spaces[GATE_MAX_SPACES];\nstatic int nspaces;\nstatic struct gate_space *cur;\nstatic const struct gate_sched *sched;\n\nstatic unsigned long win_lo, win_hi;\t/* the same virtual window in every space */\nstatic unsigned long brk_lo, brk_cur, map_lo;\t/* a Linux program's heap: brk grows up from brk_lo, mmap2 down from map_lo; 0 for the others */\nstatic const struct door *calls;\n\n/* Is [p, p+n) all inside what ring 3 may touch? */\nstatic int user_ok(unsigned long p, unsigned long n)\n{\n\tunsigned long e = p + n;\n\n\tif (n == 0)\n\t\treturn 1;\n\tif (e < p)\n\t\treturn 0;\n\tif (p >= win_lo && e <= win_hi)\n\t\treturn 1;\n\treturn cur->user_stack && p >= (unsigned long)gate_stack_lo && e <= (unsigned long)gate_stack_hi;\n}\n\n/* Is the NUL-terminated string at p entirely inside what ring 3 may touch? */\nstatic int user_name_ok(const char *s)\n{\n\tunsigned long i;\n\n\tfor (i = 0; i < NAME_MAX_LEN; i++) {\n\t\tif (!user_ok((unsigned long)s + i, 1))\n\t\t\treturn 0;\n\t\tif (!s[i])\n\t\t\treturn 1;\n\t}\n\treturn 0;\n}\n\n/* A Linux program's open files: the disk's root directory, read-only.  Each is\n * read whole when it is opened (the file system as lifted reads whole files) and\n * handed out from there. */\nstatic struct {\n\tint used;\n\tunsigned long len, pos;\n\tunsigned char data[FILE_MAX];\n} files[NFILES];\n\nstatic long file_open(unsigned long path, unsigned long flags)\n{\n\tconst char *name = (const char *)path;\n\tlong r;\n\tint i;\n\n\tif (!brk_lo)\n\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\n\tif (!user_name_ok(name))\n\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\tif ((flags & 3) != 0 || (flags & 0100))\n\t\treturn -EROFS;\t\t/* written to, or made: the disk is read-only to a Linux program */\n\tif (*name == '/')\n\t\tname++;\n\tfor (r = 0; name[r]; r++)\n\t\tif (name[r] == '/')\n\t\t\treturn -ENOENT;\t/* only the root directory */\n\tif (!*name)\n\t\treturn -ENOENT;\n\tfor (i = 0; i < NFILES && files[i].used; i++)\n\t\t;\n\tif (i == NFILES)\n\t\treturn -EMFILE;\n\tr = calls->load(name, files[i].data, FILE_MAX);\n\tif (r < 0)\n\t\treturn -ENOENT;\n\tfiles[i].used = 1;\n\tfiles[i].len = (unsigned long)r > FILE_MAX ? FILE_MAX : (unsigned long)r;\n\tfiles[i].pos = 0;\n\treturn 3 + i;\n}\n\nstatic int file_ok(unsigned long fd)\n{\n\treturn fd >= 3 && fd < 3 + NFILES && files[fd - 3].used;\n}\n\nstatic long file_read(unsigned long fd, unsigned char *b, unsigned long n)\n{\n\tunsigned long k, left = files[fd - 3].len - files[fd - 3].pos;\n\n\tif (n > left)\n\t\tn = left;\n\tfor (k = 0; k < n; k++)\n\t\tb[k] = files[fd - 3].data[files[fd - 3].pos + k];\n\tfiles[fd - 3].pos += n;\n\treturn (long)n;\n}\n\n/* The console as a Linux program's standard input: what is typed, up to the first\n * Ctrl+D, which ends the input the way it does at the start of a line on a Unix\n * terminal (its line discipline turns it into a read of nothing).  The Ctrl+D is\n * not handed over: what came before it in the same read is, and the next read\n * is the end.  Anything typed after it in the same read is dropped. */\nstatic int console_eof;\n\nstatic long console_read(char *b, unsigned long n)\n{\n\tunsigned long got, i;\n\n\tif (n == 0)\n\t\treturn 0;\n\tif (console_eof) {\n\t\tconsole_eof = 0;\n\t\treturn 0;\n\t}\n\tgot = calls->read(b, n);\n\tfor (i = 0; i < got; i++)\n\t\tif (b[i] == 0x04) {\n\t\t\tconsole_eof = i > 0;\n\t\t\treturn (long)i;\n\t\t}\n\treturn (long)got;\n}\n\n/* \"exit N\" on the console when a Linux program ends: its status, which nothing\n * else outside the machine could see. */\nstatic void say_exit(unsigned long code)\n{\n\tchar m[16] = \"exit \";\n\tunsigned long k = 5, v = code & 0xff, d = 100;\n\n\twhile (d > 1 && v < d)\n\t\td /= 10;\n\tfor (; d; d /= 10)\n\t\tm[k++] = (char)('0' + (v / d) % 10);\n\tm[k++] = '\\n';\n\tcalls->write(m, k);\n}\n\n/* writev(fd, iov, n): write's pieces, in order.  Only the console, as write. */\nstatic long writev(unsigned long fd, unsigned long iov, unsigned long n)\n{\n\tconst unsigned long *v = (const unsigned long *)iov;\n\tunsigned long i;\n\tlong total = 0;\n\n\tif (fd != 1 && fd != 2)\n\t\treturn -EBADF;\n\tif (n > 1024 || !user_ok(iov, n * 8))\n\t\treturn -EINVAL;\n\tfor (i = 0; i < n; i++)\n\t\tif (!user_ok(v[2 * i], v[2 * i + 1]))\n\t\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\tfor (i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n\t\tcalls->write((const void *)v[2 * i], v[2 * i + 1]);\n\t\ttotal += (long)v[2 * i + 1];\n\t}\n\treturn total;\n}\n\n/* brk(p): move the end of the heap to p, if p is between where it started and\n * the lowest mapped piece; either way answer where it is now, as Linux does. */\nstatic unsigned long brk(unsigned long p)\n{\n\tif (brk_lo && p >= brk_lo && p <= map_lo)\n\t\tbrk_cur = p;\n\treturn brk_cur;\n}\n\n/* mmap2: only fresh, private, zeroed memory (what malloc asks for), taken from\n * the top of the heap's room down; anything else is refused. */\nstatic long mmap2(unsigned long addr, unsigned long len, unsigned long flags, unsigned long fd)\n{\n\tunsigned long n = (len + PAGE - 1) & ~(PAGE - 1), i;\n\tunsigned char *p;\n\n\t(void)addr;\t\t/* where it would like it is only a hint, in Linux too */\n\tif (!brk_lo)\n\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\n\tif ((flags & (MAP_PRIVATE | MAP_ANONYMOUS)) != (MAP_PRIVATE | MAP_ANONYMOUS) || (long)fd != -1)\n\t\treturn -EINVAL;\n\tif (n == 0 || n < len || n > map_lo - brk_cur)\n\t\treturn -ENOMEM;\n\tmap_lo -= n;\n\tp = (unsigned char *)map_lo;\n\tfor (i = 0; i < n; i++)\n\t\tp[i] = 0;\n\treturn (long)map_lo;\n}\n\n/* set_thread_area(u_info): one segment for the program's thread storage, GDT\n * entry 6, at the base it asks for, 4 GiB long, ring 3, data.  Linux's struct\n * user_desc: entry number (-1: choose), base, limit, flags. */\nstatic long set_thread_area(unsigned long u)\n{\n\tunsigned long *d = (unsigned long *)u, base;\n\n\tif (!brk_lo)\n\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\n\tif (!user_ok(u, 16))\n\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\tif (d[0] != (unsigned long)-1 && d[0] != TLS_ENTRY)\n\t\treturn -EINVAL;\n\tbase = d[1];\n\tgdt[TLS_ENTRY] = 0xffffULL | ((unsigned long long)(base & 0xffffffUL) << 16) | (0xf2ULL << 40) |\n\t\t(0xfULL << 48) | (0xcULL << 52) | ((unsigned long long)(base >> 24) << 56);\n\td[0] = TLS_ENTRY;\n\treturn 0;\n}\n\n/* statx(fd, \"\", AT_EMPTY_PATH, mask, buf): what the console is, for the three\n * descriptors a program starts with (a character device, no size, a 1 KiB block),\n * or an open file (a regular file, read-only, its size).  Linux's struct statx,\n * 256 bytes; only the fields cat reads are set. */\nstatic long statx(unsigned long fd, unsigned long path, unsigned long flags, unsigned long buf)\n{\n\tunsigned char *b = (unsigned char *)buf;\n\tunsigned long i;\n\n\tif (!brk_lo)\n\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\n\tif (!user_name_ok((const char *)path) || !user_ok(buf, 256))\n\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\tif (!(flags & AT_EMPTY_PATH) || ((const char *)path)[0])\n\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\t\t/* by name: not yet */\n\tif (fd > 2 && !file_ok(fd))\n\t\treturn -EBADF;\n\tfor (i = 0; i < 256; i++)\n\t\tb[i] = 0;\n\t*(unsigned int *)(b + 0) = 0x7ff;\t\t/* stx_mask: the basic fields */\n\t*(unsigned int *)(b + 4) = 1024;\t\t/* stx_blksize */\n\t*(unsigned int *)(b + 16) = 1;\t\t\t/* stx_nlink */\n\tif (fd <= 2) {\n\t\t*(unsigned short *)(b + 28) = 0020620;\t/* stx_mode: the console, a character device, rw--w---- */\n\t} else {\n\t\t*(unsigned short *)(b + 28) = 0100444;\t/* a file, r--r--r-- */\n\t\t*(unsigned long *)(b + 40) = files[fd - 3].len;\t/* stx_size, low half */\n\t}\n\treturn 0;\n}\n\n/* frame: what trap_common saved (gate_entry.S).  Returns the call's answer. */\nstatic long syscall(const unsigned long *frame)\n{\n\tunsigned long nr = frame[7], a = frame[4], b = frame[6], c = frame[5];\t/* eax; ebx, ecx, edx */\n\n\tswitch (nr) {\n\tcase NR_EXIT:\n\tcase NR_EXIT_GROUP:\n\t\tif (brk_lo)\n\t\t\tsay_exit(a);\t\t/* a Linux program's status, where a shell would show it */\n\t\tcalls->exit((int)a);\n\tcase NR_WRITE:\n\t\tif (a != 1 && a != 2)\n\t\t\treturn -EBADF;\n\t\tif (!user_ok(b, c))\n\t\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\t\tcalls->write((const void *)b, c);\n\t\treturn (long)c;\n\tcase NR_READ:\n\t\tif (brk_lo && file_ok(a)) {\n\t\t\tif (!user_ok(b, c))\n\t\t\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\t\t\treturn file_read(a, (unsigned char *)b, c);\n\t\t}\n\t\tif (a != 0)\n\t\t\treturn -EBADF;\n\t\tif (!user_ok(b, c))\n\t\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\t\tif (brk_lo)\n\t\t\treturn console_read((char *)b, c);\n\t\treturn (long)calls->read((void *)b, c);\n\tcase NR_ALLOC:\n\t\tif (brk_lo)\n\t\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\t\t/* Linux programs get -ENOSYS at 1000; old-table programs keep alloc */\n\t\treturn (long)calls->alloc(a);\n\tcase NR_MS:\n\t\tif (brk_lo)\n\t\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\t\t/* Linux programs get -ENOSYS at 1001; old-table programs keep ms */\n\t\treturn (long)calls->ms();\n\tcase NR_LOAD:\n\t\tif (brk_lo)\n\t\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\t\t/* Linux programs get -ENOSYS at 1002; old-table programs keep load */\n\t\tif (!user_name_ok((const char *)a) || !user_ok(b, c))\n\t\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\t\treturn calls->load((const char *)a, (void *)b, c);\n\tcase NR_SAVE:\n\t\tif (brk_lo)\n\t\t\treturn -ENOSYS;\t\t/* Linux programs get -ENOSYS at 1003; old-table programs keep save */\n\t\tif (!user_name_ok((const char *)a) || !user_ok(b, c))\n\t\t\treturn -EFAULT;\n\t\treturn calls->save((const char *)a, (const void *)b, c);\n\tcase NR_OPEN:\n\t\treturn file_open(a, b);\n\tcase NR_CLOSE:\n\t\tif (brk_lo && file_ok(a)) {\n\t\t\tfiles[a - 3].used = 0;\n\t\t\treturn 0;\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn a <= 2 ? 0 : -EBADF;\n\tcase NR_IOCTL:\n\t\treturn a <= 2 || (brk_lo && file_ok(a)) ? -ENOTTY : -EBADF;\t/* neither the console nor a file is a terminal the program may steer */\n\tcase NR_WRITEV:\n\t\treturn writev(a, b, c);\n\tcase NR_BRK:\n\t\treturn (long)brk(a);\n\tcase NR_MMAP2:\n\t\treturn mmap2(a, b, frame[1], frame[0]);\t/* addr, length; flags in esi, fd in edi (frame: edi esi ebp esp ebx edx ecx eax) */\n\tcase NR_MUNMAP:\n\t\treturn brk_lo ? 0 : -ENOSYS;\t/* nothing given back: the memory stays the program's until it exits */\n\tcase NR_SET_THREAD_AREA:\n\t\treturn set_thread_area(a);\n\tcase NR_SET_TID_ADDRESS:\n\t\treturn brk_lo ? 1 : -ENOSYS;\t/* one program, one thread: its number is 1 */\n\tcase NR_STATX:\n\t\treturn statx(a, b, c, frame[0]);\t/* dirfd, path, flags; the mask in esi is ignored, the answer goes to edi's address */\n\t}\n\treturn -ENOSYS;\n}\n\nstatic void hex8(char *p, unsigned long v)\n{\n\tint i;\n\n\tfor (i = 7; i >= 0; i--, v >>= 4)\n\t\tp[i] = \"0123456789ABCDEF\"[v & 15];\n}\n\n/*\n * Every trap.  frame points at what trap_common saved: eight registers (popal\n * order, edi first), es, ds, the vector, the error code, then what the\n * processor pushed.  Returns the frame to resume.\n */\nunsigned long *gate_trap(unsigned long *frame)\n{\n\tunsigned long vec = frame[10], addr;\n\tstruct gate_space *next = cur;\n\tstatic char msg[] = \"\\nGuru Meditation #XXXXXXXX.XXXXXXXX  program 1 stopped\\n\";\n\n\tcur->frame = frame;\n\tif (vec == 0x80) {\n\t\tframe[7] = (unsigned long)syscall(frame);\n\t\treturn frame;\n\t}\n\tif (sched && ((vec & ~0xfUL) == 0x30 || vec == 0xff)) {\n\t\t/* a hardware interrupt: the scheduler may take the processor away */\n\t\tnext = sched->irq(vec, cur);\n\t} else {\n\t\t/* The processor refused something.  The two numbers are the vector and the\n\t\t * address: the one it touched (page faults) or the instruction it was on. */\n\t\tif (vec == 14)\n\t\t\t__asm__ volatile(\"movl %%cr2, %0\" : \"=r\"(addr));\n\t\telse\n\t\t\taddr = frame[12];\n\t\tif (sched && (frame[13] & 3) == 3) {\n\t\t\tnext = sched->fault(vec, addr, cur);\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\thex8(msg + 18, vec);\n\t\t\thex8(msg + 27, addr);\n\t\t\tkernel_say(msg, sizeof msg - 1);\n\t\t\tkernel_flush();\n\t\t\tkernel_halt();\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif (next != cur) {\n\t\tcur = next;\n\t\ttss[1] = (unsigned int)next->kstack_top;\n\t\t__asm__ volatile(\"movl %0, %%cr3\" : : \"r\"(next->cr3) : \"memory\");\n\t}\n\treturn next->frame;\n}\n\nstatic void set_gate(int v, const char *handler, unsigned int dpl)\n{\n\tunsigned long h = (unsigned long)handler;\n\n\t/* present, 32-bit interrupt gate (interrupts off on entry), code selector 0x08 */\n\tidt[v] = (h & 0xffff) | ((unsigned long long)SEL_KCODE << 16) |\n\t\t((unsigned long long)(0x8e00 | (dpl << 13) | (h & 0xffff0000)) << 32);\n}\n\nstatic void tables_init(void)\n{\n\tstatic struct { unsigned short limit; unsigned int base; } __attribute__((packed)) gd, id;\n\textern char trap0[], trap1[], trap2[], trap3[], trap4[], trap5[], trap6[], trap7[],\n\t\ttrap8[], trap9[], trap10[], trap11[], trap12[], trap13[], trap14[], trap15[],\n\t\ttrap16[], trap17[], trap18[], trap19[], trap20[], trap21[], trap22[], trap23[],\n\t\ttrap24[], trap25[], trap26[], trap27[], trap28[], trap29[], trap30[], trap31[],\n\t\ttrap0x80[];\n\tchar *t[32] = { trap0, trap1, trap2, trap3, trap4, trap5, trap6, trap7,\n\t\ttrap8, trap9, trap10, trap11, trap12, trap13, trap14, trap15,\n\t\ttrap16, trap17, trap18, trap19, trap20, trap21, trap22, trap23,\n\t\ttrap24, trap25, trap26, trap27, trap28, trap29, trap30, trap31 };\n\tunsigned long base = (unsigned long)tss, limit = sizeof tss - 1;\n\tint i;\n\n\tgdt[0] = 0;\n\tgdt[1] = 0x00cf9a000000ffffULL;\t\t/* kernel code: base 0, 4 GiB, ring 0 */\n\tgdt[2] = 0x00cf92000000ffffULL;\t\t/* kernel data */\n\tgdt[3] = 0x00cffa000000ffffULL;\t\t/* user code: the same, ring 3 */\n\tgdt[4] = 0x00cff2000000ffffULL;\t\t/* user data */\n\tgdt[5] = (limit & 0xffff) | ((unsigned long long)(base & 0xffffffUL) << 16) | (0x89ULL << 40) |\n\t\t((unsigned long long)(limit >> 16) << 48) | ((unsigned long long)(base >> 24) << 56);\n\n\ttss[1] = (unsigned int)stack_top;\t/* esp0: where a trap from ring 3 lands */\n\ttss[2] = SEL_KDATA;\t\t\t/* ss0 */\n\ttss[25] = sizeof tss << 16;\t\t/* no I/O bitmap: every port is closed to ring 3 */\n\n\tgd.limit = sizeof gdt - 1;\n\tgd.base = (unsigned int)gdt;\n\t__asm__ volatile(\"lgdt %0; movl %1, %%eax; movl %%eax, %%ds; movl %%eax, %%es; movl %%eax, %%ss; \"\n\t\t\"movl %%eax, %%fs; movl %%eax, %%gs; ljmp %2, $1f; 1:\"\n\t\t: : \"m\"(gd), \"i\"(SEL_KDATA), \"i\"(SEL_KCODE) : \"eax\", \"memory\");\n\t__asm__ volatile(\"ltr %%ax\" : : \"a\"(SEL_TSS));\n\n\tfor (i = 0; i < 32; i++)\n\t\tset_gate(i, t[i], 0);\n\tset_gate(0x80, trap0x80, 3);\t\t/* the one gate a program may knock on */\n\tif (sched) {\n\t\t/* hardware interrupts: the 8259's sixteen vectors from 0x30, and the APIC's spurious one */\n\t\textern char *trap_irq_table[17];\n\t\tfor (i = 0; i < 16; i++)\n\t\t\tset_gate(0x30 + i, trap_irq_table[i], 0);\n\t\tset_gate(0xff, trap_irq_table[16], 0);\n\t}\n\tid.limit = sizeof idt - 1;\n\tid.base = (unsigned int)idt;\n\t__asm__ volatile(\"lidt %0\" : : \"m\"(id));\n}\n\n/* The first 4 MiB, page by page: the kernel, closed to ring 3, but for the stub page. */\nstatic void base_init(int user_stack)\n{\n\tunsigned long i, stub = (unsigned long)gate_user_start >> 12;\n\n\tfor (i = 0; i < 1024; i++)\n\t\tpt0[i] = (i << 12) | 0x3;\t/* present, writable, kernel only */\n\tpt0[stub] = (stub << 12) | 0x5;\t\t/* present, read-only, ring 3 may read and run it */\n\tif (user_stack)\n\t\tfor (i = (unsigned long)gate_stack_lo >> 12; i < (unsigned long)gate_stack_hi >> 12; i++)\n\t\t\tpt0[i] |= 0x4;\t\t/* ring 3's stack, when it stands on the gate's */\n}\n\n/* A page directory: every address maps to itself, closed to ring 3, except that\n * the window [virt, virt + len) maps to physical memory from PHYS and is open.\n * TECH-DEBT 3: the window is rounded up to whole 4 MiB pages, so a program can\n * reach the tail past its end.  It goes in the mechanic, after the series. */\nstatic void space_map(unsigned long *pd, unsigned long phys, unsigned long virt, unsigned long len)\n{\n\tunsigned long i, n = (len + 0x3fffffUL) >> 22;\n\n\tpd[0] = (unsigned long)pt0 | 0x7;\t/* the first 4 MiB: page by page */\n\tfor (i = 1; i < 1024; i++)\n\t\tpd[i] = (i << 22) | 0x83;\t/* 4 MiB pages, present, writable, kernel only */\n\tfor (i = 0; i < n; i++)\n\t\tpd[(virt >> 22) + i] = (phys + (i << 22)) | 0x87;\t/* the program's window: ring 3 may use it */\n}\n\nstatic void paging_on(unsigned long cr3)\n{\n\t__asm__ volatile(\"movl %%cr4, %%eax; orl $0x10, %%eax; movl %%eax, %%cr4\" : : : \"eax\");\n\t__asm__ volatile(\"movl %0, %%cr3\" : : \"r\"(cr3));\n\t__asm__ volatile(\"movl %%cr0, %%eax; orl $0x80010000, %%eax; movl %%eax, %%cr0\" : : : \"eax\");\n}\n\nvoid __attribute__((noreturn)) gate_enter(unsigned long eip, unsigned long esp, unsigned long eflags);\nvoid __attribute__((noreturn)) gate_resume_frame(unsigned long cr3, unsigned long *frame);\n\n/* the kernel and the stub page must fit under the first 4 MiB, and the window must start above it */\nstatic int window_ok(unsigned long lo, unsigned long hi)\n{\n\treturn (unsigned long)kernel_end <= 0x400000 && lo >= 0x400000 && hi >= lo && hi <= 0xffc00000UL;\n}\n\nvoid __attribute__((noreturn)) gate_run(unsigned long entry, unsigned long lo, unsigned long hi,\n\tconst struct door *kernel_calls)\n{\n\tunsigned long *sp, top = (unsigned long)gate_stack_hi;\n\tstruct gate_space *s = &spaces[0];\n\n\tif (!window_ok(lo, hi))\n\t\tkernel_halt();\n\tcalls = kernel_calls;\n\twin_lo = lo;\n\twin_hi = hi;\n\tnspaces = 1;\n\tbase_init(1);\n\tspace_map(pds[0], lo, lo, hi - lo);\n\ts->cr3 = (unsigned long)pds[0];\n\ts->kstack_top = (unsigned long)stack_top;\n\ts->user_stack = 1;\n\tcur = s;\n\ttables_init();\n\tpaging_on(s->cr3);\n\n\t__asm__(\"\" : \"+r\"(top));\t\t/* the stack is a place the linker chose, not an object the compiler may reason about */\n\tsp = (unsigned long *)top;\n\t*--sp = (unsigned long)gate_door;\t/* _start's argument */\n\t*--sp = (unsigned long)gate_return;\t/* where _start returns to: exit */\n\tgate_enter(entry, (unsigned long)sp, 0x002);\t/* interrupts off, I/O privilege 0 */\n}\n\n/*\n * A program from a Linux program file (PART.loader@10): its window [VIRT, VIRT +\n * LEN) is the physical RAM from PHYS, open to ring 3; it starts at ENTRY with its\n * stack at ESP (inside the window, laid out by the loader the way Linux lays it\n * out), and its heap starts at BRK.  No system call table is handed over: it\n * knocks on int 0x80 itself, as Linux programs do.\n */\nvoid __attribute__((noreturn)) gate_exec(unsigned long entry, unsigned long phys, unsigned long virt,\n\tunsigned long len, unsigned long esp, unsigned long brk_start, const struct door *kernel_calls)\n{\n\tstruct gate_space *s = &spaces[0];\n\n\tif (!window_ok(virt, virt + len) || brk_start < virt || brk_start > virt + len)\n\t\tkernel_halt();\n\tcalls = kernel_calls;\n\twin_lo = virt;\n\twin_hi = virt + len;\n\tbrk_lo = brk_cur = brk_start;\n\tmap_lo = esp & ~(PAGE - 1);\n\tif (map_lo > 0x100000UL + brk_lo)\n\t\tmap_lo -= 0x100000UL;\t\t/* the stack keeps 1 MiB below where it starts */\n\tnspaces = 1;\n\tbase_init(0);\n\tspace_map(pds[0], phys, virt, len);\n\ts->cr3 = (unsigned long)pds[0];\n\ts->kstack_top = (unsigned long)stack_top;\n\ts->user_stack = 0;\n\tcur = s;\n\ttables_init();\n\tpaging_on(s->cr3);\n\tgate_enter(entry, esp, 0x002);\t/* interrupts off, I/O privilege 0 */\n}\n\nvoid gate_share_calls(const struct door *kernel_calls)\n{\n\tcalls = kernel_calls;\n}\n\nvoid gate_schedule(const struct gate_sched *s)\n{\n\tsched = s;\n}\n\nstruct gate_space *gate_space_new(unsigned long phys, unsigned long virt, unsigned long len,\n\tunsigned long entry, unsigned long stack_top_virt)\n{\n\tstruct gate_space *s;\n\tunsigned long *f, *us;\n\n\tif (nspaces >= GATE_MAX_SPACES || !window_ok(virt, virt + len))\n\t\treturn 0;\n\tif (nspaces && (virt != win_lo || virt + len != win_hi))\n\t\treturn 0;\t\t\t/* one window for every space */\n\tif (!nspaces) {\n\t\twin_lo = virt;\n\t\twin_hi = virt + len;\n\t\tbase_init(0);\n\t}\n\ts = &spaces[nspaces];\n\tspace_map(pds[nspaces], phys, virt, len);\n\ts->cr3 = (unsigned long)pds[nspaces];\n\ts->kstack_top = (unsigned long)&kstacks[nspaces][KSTACK_WORDS];\n\ts->user_stack = 0;\n\n\t/* the program's stack, written through its physical address (paging is off): _start's argument and return */\n\tus = (unsigned long *)(phys + (stack_top_virt - virt));\n\t*--us = (unsigned long)gate_door;\n\t*--us = (unsigned long)gate_return;\n\n\t/* and the frame that resumes it, as if it had been trapped just before its first instruction */\n\tf = (unsigned long *)s->kstack_top;\n\t*--f = 0x23;\t\t\t\t/* ss */\n\t*--f = stack_top_virt - 8;\t\t/* esp */\n\t*--f = 0x202;\t\t\t\t/* eflags: interrupts on, I/O privilege 0 */\n\t*--f = 0x1b;\t\t\t\t/* cs */\n\t*--f = entry;\t\t\t\t/* eip */\n\t*--f = 0;\t\t\t\t/* error code */\n\t*--f = 0;\t\t\t\t/* vector */\n\t*--f = 0x23;\t\t\t\t/* ds */\n\t*--f = 0x23;\t\t\t\t/* es */\n\t{\n\t\tint k;\n\n\t\tfor (k = 0; k < 8; k++)\n\t\t\t*--f = 0;\t\t/* edi esi ebp esp ebx edx ecx eax */\n\t}\n\ts->frame = f;\n\tnspaces++;\n\treturn s;\n}\n\nvoid __attribute__((noreturn)) gate_resume(struct gate_space *s)\n{\n\tcur = s;\n\ttss[1] = (unsigned int)s->kstack_top;\n\tgate_resume_frame(s->cr3, s->frame);\n}\n\nvoid __attribute__((noreturn)) gate_start(struct gate_space *first)\n{\n\tcur = first;\n\ttables_init();\n\tpaging_on(first->cr3);\n\tgate_resume(first);\n}\n"
